| `/self-defense-classes-for-women/` | **2026-02-26** | women |
| plus the eleven-page general self-defence cluster | — | everyone |
| plus `/kids-self-defense-classes-near-me/` | 2026-02-26 | children |
| **programme pages for any of it** | — | **none** |

**Heading overlap:**

| Sibling | Exact H2 matches | All h2+h3 | H2 word overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/womens-self-defense-class/` | 1 of 10 (10.0%, "Conclusion") | 3 of 31 (9.7%) | **66.7%** |
| `/self-defense-classes-near-me/` | 1 of 10 (10.0%, "Contact US") | 3 of 31 (9.7%) | 62.3% |
| `/best-self-defense/` | **0 of 10** | 3 of 31 (9.7%) | 37.7% |

**66.7% heading-word overlap with the existing women's post** on a ten-heading
page, with only boilerplate matching exactly — the familiar pattern of one
template with the keyword swapped.

**Marked CRITICAL for a different reason from the other clusters.** Elsewhere the
problem is that several pages compete for one query. Here the problem is that
**two pages compete to sell a class the homepage says does not exist yet**
(issue 39). Consolidation is the second question; the first is whether either
page should be live at all.

**If the programme launches:** keep this page, which is the better of the two — it
is longer, it states the women-only policy explicitly, and its "Who Can Join"
section (beginners, students and professionals, teen girls, mothers) is the
clearest audience segmentation on the site. 301 `/womens-self-defense-class/` into
it, add a programme page, and fix the uncited statistics (issue 62) and the two
FAQ answers on lethal force and concealed blades (issue 31) first.

**If it does not launch:** both pages should come down, along with the "Women's
Self-Defense Programs" and "Women Only Classes" claims on `/martial-arts-near-me/`
and `/martial-arts-gyms-near-me/`.

#### The Muay Thai benefits trio, and the mildest overlap in this document

`/benefits-of-muay-thai/` (7 March 2026, 2,814 words) is the third post on what
Muay Thai training does for you:

| Page | Published | Angle |
|---|---|---|
| `/muay-thai-training/` | — | what training involves |
| `/muay-thai-fitness-workout/` | — | the workout and conditioning side |
| `/benefits-of-muay-thai/` | **2026-03-07** | **a numbered top-ten of benefits** |

**Heading overlap:**

| Sibling | Exact H2 matches | All h2+h3 | H2 word overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/muay-thai-training/` | 2 of 16 (12.5%, "Conclusion" and "FAQ's") | 2 of 26 (7.7%) | **43.8%** |
| `/muay-thai-fitness-workout/` | 2 of 16 (12.5%, the same two) | 2 of 26 (7.7%) | 20.5% |

**The closest pair is `/benefits-of-muay-thai/` and `/muay-thai-training/`** at
43.8% heading-word overlap — but that is the mildest figure for any confirmed
cluster in this document, against 89.8% for the two adult Muay Thai posts and
82.3% for the two kids pages. The numbered-list format genuinely differentiates
this one: ten `1.`-to-`10.` H2s is a distinct page shape that none of the other
Muay Thai posts uses.

**The ten benefits are ten distinct items with no duplicates and no padding** —
Full Body Workout, Effective Weight Loss, Improved Cardiovascular Health,
Practical Self Defense Skills, Increased Strength and Power, Better Balance and
Coordination, Stress Relief and Mental Focus, Boosted Confidence, Discipline and
Mental Toughness, Supportive Training Community. A summary bullet list at the top
repeats the same ten in the same order, which is a legitimate table of contents
rather than padding.

**That matters because three earlier "top N" posts failed exactly this check:**
`/kickboxing-techniques/` listed the hook twice in a Top 9,
`/top-ten-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` padded ten slots with five disciplines
repeated as age-group variants, and `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` needed the same
scrutiny. **This one counts correctly.**

**Recommendation: keep all three but give them distinct jobs.** This page is the
best written of the Muay Thai set — no invented statistics, no safety overclaim,
no kids-class invention, no pricing promise (see issue 39) — and the numbered
format suits featured snippets. Merge `/muay-thai-fitness-workout/` into it if
anything is cut, since its workout content is a subset of benefits 1 to 3.

#### The Muay Thai origins cluster: six pages telling one story

`/history-of-muay-thai/` (8 March 2026, 3,561 words) is the sixth page on where
Muay Thai came from:

| Page | Angle |
|---|---|
| `/what-is-muay-thai/` | what the art is, with an origin section |
| `/muay-boran/` | the ancient predecessor |
| `/muay-thai-muay-boran/` | the two compared |
| `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` | the regional styles |
| `/muay-chaiya/` | one regional style in depth |
| `/history-of-muay-thai/` | **the chronological history, era by era** |

**Heading overlap is low, and lower than the word overlap suggests:**

| Sibling | Exact H2 matches | All levels | H2 word overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/muay-boran/` | 1 of 14 (7.1%, "Conclusion") | 3 of 36 (8.3%) | **57.6%** |
| `/what-is-muay-thai/` | 1 of 14 (7.1%, "Conclusion") | 1 of 36 (2.8%) | 55.3% |

**The closest pair is `/history-of-muay-thai/` and `/muay-boran/`** at 57.6%
heading-word overlap — both tell the ancient-origins story, both cover Rama-era
modernisation, and both end on cultural significance. But the structures differ
genuinely: this page is organised by **dynasty** (Sukhothai → Ayutthaya →
Rattanakosin → modern → global → international → cultural), which none of the
other five uses.

**The duplication problem here is not structural, it is factual** — six pages
telling one story with incompatible dates and kings (issue 91). Consolidating
would fix both at once.

**Recommendation: keep `/history-of-muay-thai/` as the origins page** — it is the
best organised and the only one with a chronology — and 301 `/muay-boran/` and
`/muay-thai-muay-boran/` into it after merging their regional-style content, which
this page is missing entirely. Keep `/muay-chaiya/` and
`/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` only if the client wants
style-specific pages, and fix the 16th-century Nai Khanom Tom error on the latter
before anything else.

#### The benefits twins, and a nine-page kickboxing cluster with no kickboxing programme

`/benefits-of-kickboxing/` (8 March 2026, 10:46) and `/benefits-of-muay-thai/`
(7 March 2026, 12:03) were published **22 hours apart and are template twins**:

| Sibling | Exact H2 matches | All levels | H2 word overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/benefits-of-muay-thai/` | 5 of 16 (31.3%) | 5 of 22 (22.7%) | **67.1%** |
| `/kick-boxing-classes/` | 1 of 16 (6.3%, "FAQ's") | 2 of 22 (9.1%) | 7.6% |

**Benefit 1 is byte-identical on both pages: "1. Full Body Workout".** Both run a
ten-item numbered H2 list preceded by the same ten items as a bullet summary, both
end with the same Why-Choose section, and the benefit sequence tracks closely —
full body workout, weight loss, cardiovascular health, strength, then the
confidence and discipline items. The structure is one template with the discipline
swapped.

**Both number their ten benefits correctly.** Ten distinct items, no duplicates, no
padding, and the bullet summary repeats the same ten in the same order. That
matters because `/kickboxing-techniques/` listed **the hook twice in a Top 9**, so
this cluster has previous. **The count is right on this page.**

**The kickboxing cluster now runs to nine pages across two slug spellings**, and
**kickboxing has no programme page at all** — it maps to Muay Thai:

| Page | Subject |
|---|---|
| `/benefits-of-kickboxing/` | benefits |
| `/kick-boxing-classes/` | classes |
| `/kickboxing-techniques/` | techniques (the Top 9 with a repeat) |
| `/kickboxing-gloves/` · `/kick-boxing-gloves/` | **the same keyword on two URLs differing by one hyphen** (already CRITICAL) |
| `/kick-boxing-bag/` | equipment |
| `/muay-thai-vs-kickboxing/` · `/mma-vs-kickboxing-for-self-defense/` · `/mixed-martial-arts-vs-kickboxing/` | three comparison posts |

**Recommendation.** Keep `/benefits-of-kickboxing/` as the kickboxing explainer —
it is the cleanest of the nine and the only one with a usable structure — but
point it at the Muay Thai programme, which is what a reader who converts will
actually book. Resolve the gloves pair first (already logged), then merge
`/kick-boxing-classes/` into the Muay Thai programme page, since it advertises a
class that does not exist as a separate offering.

#### The comparison-post group

Three posts now compare the same three disciplines in rotating pairs:

| URL | Pair | Words | Published |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/muay-thai-vs-kickboxing/` | Muay Thai vs Kickboxing | 2,586 | 2025-05 |
| `/muay-thai-vs-mma/` | Muay Thai vs MMA | 2,262 | 2025-07 |
| `/mma-vs-kickboxing-for-self-defense/` | MMA vs Kickboxing | **6,785** | 2025-09 |

Between them they cover all three pairings of Muay Thai, MMA and kickboxing, and
each repeats the others' ground: what each art is, where it came from, how the
striking differs, which is better for self-defence. The newest is nearly three
times the length of either of the others and covers both of their topics inside
itself — it has sections on Muay Thai's influence on kickboxing and on MMA's
ground game, which are the subjects of the other two posts.

**It also joins the self-defence cluster.** Its title, its primary keyword and
five of its seventeen sections are about self-defence, so it competes with
`/best-martial-arts/`, `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` and
`/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/` (the CRITICAL trio above) as well
as with the two comparison posts. It links two of those three itself.

**What to do:** the newest post is long enough and complete enough to absorb
both of the older comparisons. Merge `/muay-thai-vs-kickboxing/` and
`/muay-thai-vs-mma/` into it and 301 them, or — if the client wants separate
short comparison pages — cut the newest one down so it stops covering their
topics. The current arrangement has one 6,785-word page competing with two
2,500-word pages that it already contains.

#### The self-defence septet, and a spelling that makes it worse — **CRITICAL**

Seven posts now answer the same question:

| URL | Title | Published |
|---|---|---|
| `/best-martial-arts/` | "Best Martial Arts for Self-Defense: Which Style Suits You?" | 2025-01-21 |
| `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | "Best Martial Arts for Self-Defense: Top Disciplines Ranked" | 2025-02-18 |
| `/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/` | "Which Martial Art is Best for Self-Defence?" | 2025-09-08 |
| `/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/` | "Best Fighting Style for Self Defense" | 2025-09-17, 10:26 |
| `/best-self-defense/` | "Best Self Defense Martial Arts for Protection" | 2025-09-17, 11:02 |
| `/top-ten-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | "Top Ten Martial Arts for Self defense at Sixth Sense Martial Arts" | 2025-09-20 |
| `/martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | "Best Martial Arts for Self Defense" | 2025-10-18 |

The first two were already logged as a pair. The third, published seven months
later, asks the identical question in a different grammatical shape. **The
fourth, nine days after the third, asks it again** — "best fighting style" is
"best martial art" with one noun swapped, and its own FAQ opens with the
question "**What is the best martial art for self defense?**", which is the
second post's exact title.

**The fifth was published thirty-six minutes after the fourth.** Not thirty-six
days — thirty-six minutes. `/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/` went live at
10:26 on 17 September 2025 and `/best-self-defense/` at 11:02 the same morning.
Its H1 is "Best Self Defense **Martial Arts** for Protection", so it is not even
a different noun this time; it is the same three words in a different order.

The two are near-identical in construction as well as subject. Both open on
choosing a martial art for protection, both run one section each on Brazilian
Jiu Jitsu, Muay Thai and MMA, both carry a "Why Choose Sixth Sense MMA" section,
a numbered benefits list, the same Google reviews and the same Facebook-only
social block. Where the fourth runs to 2,392 words, the fifth runs to 1,858 —
the shorter, thinner version of the page published half an hour earlier.

And the only thing distinguishing the third URL from the second is **British
spelling**:

```
/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/     ← American "defense"
/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/  ← British "defence"
```

**That is the worst possible way to differentiate two pages.** Google treats
"defense" and "defence" as the same query for a US searcher, so the two pages
compete directly; but a person who types the British spelling and a person who
types the American one are the same customer in Coppell, Texas, where nobody
writes "defence". The site has produced two URLs for one question and
distinguished them by a spelling its own audience does not use.

**The fourth page links two of the other three.** `/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/`
links "best martial art for self defense" to `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`
and "Best mart[ial] art for self defense" to `/best-martial-arts/`. As with the
BJJ cluster (issue 57), the newest page cites its own direct competitors as
sources — which is the clearest possible demonstration that these should be one
page.

**The fifth links none of them**, which is arguably worse: it sits in the middle
of a five-page cluster and points instead at `/womens-self-defense-class/`,
`/self-defense-classes-near-me/` and `/different-types-of-martial-arts/`. Nothing
on the site tells a reader or a crawler which of the five pages is the answer.

**The sixth, three days later, links three of the other five.**
`/top-ten-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` (20 September) points at
`/best-martial-arts/`, `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` and
`/best-self-defense/` — the three pages it is competing with most directly. At
3,465 words it is also the longest of the six, and its subject is stated in its
own H2: "**Top ten martial arts for self defense**". That is the cluster's
question with a number in front of it.

It is the strongest page of the six on local signal (Coppell three times in the
body, thirty local tags) and the weakest on substance: its "top ten" is five
disciplines counted twice (issue 65) and its conclusion is keyword salad
(issue 67).

**And a seventh, four weeks later, whose H1 is the second post's title.**
`/martial-arts-for-self-defense/` (18 October) has the H1 "**Best Martial Arts
for Self Defense**" — which is, word for word, the first half of
`/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`'s title, published eight months earlier.
The slug differs by two words; the H1 does not differ at all.

**It links two of the six it is competing with**, including that one:
`/best-martial-arts/` and `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`. So the page
whose H1 duplicates another page's title also links to it.

Structurally it is the most conventional of the seven — six disciplines with a
heading each, three "how to choose" steps, four programme sections — and it is
the only one of the seven that names all three real kids programmes (issue 73).
It also names **four disciplines the gym does not teach** and its FAQ recommends
three of them (issue 39).

This is the **largest cannibalisation cluster in this document**: **seven pages,
one question**, spread across two spellings of "defence" and five phrasings of
"martial art". Between them they run to roughly **19,500 words** answering
something a single page should answer once — four published inside four days in
September, the seventh four weeks later.

Set against the BJJ near-me cluster (issue 57, seven pages) it is the same size
but worse, because the near-me pages at least target different disciplines. All
seven of these target one question for one reader.

**What to do: consolidate all seven into one page with six 301 redirects.**

Check Search Console first, but on the evidence `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`
is the strongest candidate to keep: it is the plainest phrasing of the query, it
already uses the American spelling, and it is the page the other two link to as
the authority. Merge the best sections of the other three into it and redirect
them.

If the client would rather keep the newest,
`/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/` has two advantages worth noting — it
carries the site's best tag set (eleven of fifteen name Coppell, issue 30) and
its meta description targets the town. Whichever survives, **the URL must use
the American spelling**, which means `/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/`
cannot be the keeper without a slug change. See issue 58 for the site-wide
spelling problem this is part of.

**This is now the largest duplication group on the site after the near-me
cluster**, and unlike that one it needs no judgement about audience: all five
pages answer one question for one reader.

**The September pages should be merged first, and today.** Whatever the client
decides about the older three, publishing two articles on the same subject
thirty-six minutes apart — and a third three days later — is not a strategy that
anyone chose; it reads like several drafts of one brief all going live.
`/best-self-defense/` is the shortest and weakest and is the obvious one to fold
in first.

**On which to keep, now that there are seven.** `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`
remains the recommendation below: plainest phrasing, American spelling, eight
months of history, and the page the others cite. The seventh page's H1 is
already that page's title, which makes the merge unusually clean — the surviving
page keeps its own title and absorbs the newer page's structure.

**A note on which of the September three to keep.** The strongest candidate is
`/top-ten-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`, but only after issues 65 and 67 are
fixed: it is the only one of the six whose body copy names Coppell (twice), it
has the most complete structure, and its comparison table and step-by-step
section are content the others do not have. Fixed, it is the page. Unfixed, it
is the one with a padded list and a machine-written conclusion, and one of the
others should carry the cluster instead.
| Boxing vs martial arts (partial overlap) | `/what-martial-art-destroys-boxers/` · `/is-boxing-a-martial-art/` |
| **Muay Thai stance** | `/muay-thai-stance/` · `/muay-thai-fighting-stance/` |
| Muay Thai compared with another striking art (partial overlap) | `/muay-thai-vs-mma/` · `/muay-thai-vs-kickboxing/` |
| **Muay Boran (SEVEN posts, confirmed, one of them city-targeted)** | `/muay-boran/` · `/muay-boran-in-coppell/` · `/muay-thai-muay-boran/` · `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` · `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` · `/muay-chaiya/` · `/mae-mai-muay-thai-what-is-it/` — see below |

**There are now three Muay Boran posts, and the overlap has become a cluster —
raised to MEDIUM.** When this entry was written there were two. The third,
`/10-muay-boran-techniques/`, repeats what both of the others already cover:

| Covered by | `/muay-thai-muay-boran/` | `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` | `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` |
|---|---|---|---|
| What Muay Boran is | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Muay Boran vs Muay Thai | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ (its own H2 **and** a comparison table) |
| The four regional styles | mentions them | ✓ (a section each) | ✓ (a section each **and** a table) |
| Named techniques | some | some | the Top 10 list |

So all three answer "what is Muay Boran and how does it differ from Muay Thai",
and two of the three run the regional styles at length. Only the Top 10
technique list is genuinely unique to the newest post. Three URLs competing for
"muay boran" on a site this size means none of them accumulates the authority,
and the gym's internal links are already split across them.

#### The Sambo cluster reaches SIX, and THREE of them share the phrase "sambo techniques" — **CRITICAL**

`/sambo-techniques-for-beginners/` (20 January 2026) is the sixth, and it makes
the cluster's slug problem explicit. **Three of the six URLs now contain the
phrase "sambo techniques":**

| Slug | Angle | Published |
|---|---|---|
| `/sambo-techniques/` | general, with a "Sambo Academy" section and reviews | — |
| `/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/` | the same, localised to the city | 27 Dec 2025 |
| **`/sambo-techniques-for-beginners/`** | **the same, aimed at beginners, and the only one that sells a programme** | 20 Jan 2026 |
| `/sambo-martial-art/` | what Sambo is | — |
| `/sambo-moves/` | throws and locks | 13 Nov 2025 |
| `/types-of-sambo/` | the five types | 11 Nov 2025 |

**Six pages, no programme.** The qualifier pattern — bare keyword, then
`-in-coppell`, then `-for-beginners` — is the same one that produced
`/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` versus `/best-martial-arts-for-self/` and
`/kick-boxing-gloves/` versus `/kickboxing-gloves/`. It reads as someone
publishing a new post for each long-tail variation of one keyword.

**The headings were compared directly against the three closest siblings:**

| Against | H2 exact | H2+H3 exact |
|---|---|---|
| `/sambo-techniques/` | 2/12 = **17%** | 3/32 = **9%** |
| `/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/` | 3/12 = **25%** | 3/32 = **9%** |
| `/sambo-moves/` | 3/12 = **25%** | 3/32 = **9%** |
| any of the three | — | 3/32 = **9%** |

**Every single match is boilerplate** — "Contact US", "Conclusion", "Follow Us on
Social Media". At H3 level the overlap is **zero** against all three. So, again,
these are not copy-paste duplicates.

**But the subject coverage is almost total.** Throws, ground control,
submissions, fitness benefits, who-can-join and why-choose-us are each covered by
**all three** siblings as well as this page. Only "Sambo Self Defense Techniques
for Real Situations" is a section none of the three has. Six pages, one subject,
six sets of rewritten headings.

**Which to keep.** This page is the best-written of the six and the only one with
a coherent beginner curriculum — but it is also the one that makes the service
claim at issue 39, so it is the **last** one to publish, not the first. If the
programme is real, keep this page's content at `/sambo-techniques/`'s slug and
301 the other five. **If the programme is not real, this is the page to delete
first**, for the same reason.

#### The Sambo cluster reaches FIVE, and the newest is the city twin of the oldest — **CRITICAL**

`/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/` (27 December 2025) is the fifth, and it creates
the cluster's third duplication: **the same primary keyword on two slugs that
differ only by a city suffix.**

| | `/sambo-techniques/` | `/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/` |
|---|---|---|
| Primary keyword | "Sambo techniques" | "Sambo Techniques in Coppell" |
| H1 | "Sambo Techniques ... at Sixth Sense MMA" | "Sambo Techniques in Coppell Practical Grappling and Combat Skills with Sixth Sense MMA" |
| Words | — | 2,550 |
| Throws | H3s "Hip Throws and Their Execution", "Leg Reaps for Takedowns", "Sacrifice Throws Step by Step" | H2 "Throwing Techniques in Sambo" with H3s "Hip Throws and Body Rotation", "Leg Sweeps and Trips", "Shoulder Throws and Lifting Techniques" |
| Locks | "Arm Locks and Joint Manipulations", "Leg Locks for Control" | H2 "Submission Techniques in Sambo" with "Leg Locks as a Signature of Sambo", "Ankle Locks and Knee Locks" |
| Striking | "Striking Elements in Combat Sambo" | "Combat Sambo Striking Techniques" |
| Types | "Types of Sambo" | "Types of Sambo Techniques" |
| Sales section | "Sambo Academy Programs at Sixth Sense MMA", "Why Pick Sixth Sense MMA for Sambo Techniques" | "Why Choose Sixth Sense for Sambo Techniques Training in Coppell,TX" |
| Reviews | a Customer Reviews section | none |

**This is the third city/no-city pair on the site**, after
`/kick-boxing-gloves/` versus `/kickboxing-gloves/` and
`/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` versus `/bjj-belt-ranks/`. It is also the
cleanest example of the pattern, because the slugs are identical apart from
`-in-coppell`.

**The headings were compared directly, and they do NOT match verbatim.** Of this
page's 42 H2s and H3s, **3 match a heading on `/sambo-techniques/` exactly — 7%
— and all three are boilerplate** ("Contact US", "Conclusion"). At H3 level the
overlap is **zero**. So this is not a copy-paste duplicate.

**But the subjects are the same, reworded.** Seven of the twelve H2s cover
material `/sambo-techniques/` already covers under a different phrasing —
types, throws, ground and submissions, combat striking, benefits, and the "why
choose us" close. Rewritten headings over the same subject matter is harder for
the client to spot than a copy-paste and no better for search: Google still has
two pages answering "sambo techniques" and has to pick one.

**Recommendation: keep `/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/`** — the local slug is the
only thing that distinguishes any Sambo page in search — merge the academy and
reviews material from `/sambo-techniques/` into it, and 301 the other four.
**Only if the programme is real**, which is issue 39's first entry.

#### The Sambo cluster: FOUR posts, zero programme, and two overlapping pairs — **CRITICAL**

`/sambo-moves/` (13 November 2025) is the fourth. The cluster now contains two
distinct duplications rather than one:

| Pair | Overlap |
|---|---|
| `/sambo-techniques/` + `/sambo-moves/` | **The closest pair.** Both are built around throws and joint locks. `/sambo-techniques/` has H3s "Hip Throws and Their Execution", "Leg Reaps for Takedowns", "Sacrifice Throws Step by Step", "Arm Locks and Joint Manipulations", "Leg Locks for Control"; `/sambo-moves/` has "Throws", "Strong Takedowns", "Locking Moves", "Arm Lock", "Foot Sweep", "Leg Hook". Same techniques, same depth, two URLs. |
| `/sambo-martial-art/` + `/types-of-sambo/` | Both explain the five types of Sambo, as five H3s under an H2 asking the same question (detailed below). |

`/sambo-moves/` also carries an H2 "Most Effective Types Sambo Moves" and H2s for
"Sport Sambo (Only Grappling)" and "Combat Sambo (Fighting + Self-Defense)", so it
overlaps the *types* pair as well. **All four posts now cover both the types and
the techniques**, and the site competes against itself four ways on a discipline it
has no programme page for.

The four were published across six months — 20 May, 21 October, 11 November and
13 November 2025 — with the last two eight weeks apart in slug and two days apart
in date. Nothing on any of them references the others' conclusions.

**Recommendation: one page, not four.** Keep `/sambo-techniques/` as the survivor —
its technique copy is the most substantial on the subject — fold the types material
from `/sambo-martial-art/` into it as one section, and 301 `/types-of-sambo/` and
`/sambo-moves/` into it. Neither of those two has a subject the survivors do not
already cover. Note that `/types-of-sambo/` cannot be touched until issue 0 is
resolved, and all four need the service-claim answer in issue 39 first.

#### The original pair: /sambo-martial-art/ and /types-of-sambo/ answer the same question — **CRITICAL**

`/sambo-martial-art/` (20 May 2025), `/sambo-techniques/` (21 October 2025) and
`/types-of-sambo/` (11 November 2025) all target Sambo. Each of the two newer
posts links `/sambo-martial-art/`: `/sambo-techniques/` with the anchor text
"martial arts sambo's combat", `/types-of-sambo/` with "Combat sambo resembles".

As duplication this started as the ordinary pattern: two long posts on one
discipline, competing with each other, the newer one broader. At 5,271 words
`/sambo-techniques/` is the **second-longest post in the project**.

**The third post makes one pair worse than duplication.**
`/sambo-martial-art/` and `/types-of-sambo/` do not merely overlap — they
answer the same question with the same structure:

| | `/sambo-martial-art/` | `/types-of-sambo/` |
|---|---|---|
| Section heading | H2 "Types of Sambo and What Makes Each One Special" | H2 "Main Types of Sambo" |
| Subheadings | Combat Sambo · Sports Sambo · Special Sambo · Freestyle Sambo · Self-Defence Sambo | Sport Sambo · Combat Sambo · Freestyle Sambo · Self-Defense Sambo · Special Military Sambo |
| Count | five types | five types |

Same five types, as five H3s, under an H2 that asks the same thing — reordered,
and with the names re-spelled ("Sports" against "Sport", "Self-Defence" against
"Self-Defense", which is issue 58 again). `/sambo-techniques/` carries an H2
"Types of Sambo" as well, so **all three posts explain the types of Sambo**, and
the newest one is built entirely around the keyword the oldest one already had a
section for. For "types of sambo" the site now competes against itself three
ways, and the page Google is most likely to have already indexed for it is the
one that is not called that.

**This pair is the priority of the three.** `/types-of-sambo/` has no subject of
its own that `/sambo-martial-art/` does not already cover; it is a section of an
existing post, re-published as a post. It is also the page carrying issue 0, so
it needs a decision anyway.

**But consolidation is not the first question here.** Neither page should exist
in its current form, because the gym does not teach Sambo (issue 39) and the
newer one advertises a full academy for it. Merging two pages that both describe
a service the business may not offer would just produce one longer page with the
same problem.

**The order of operations matters:**

1. **Answer the Sambo question** (issue 39). Does the gym teach it or not?
2. **Decide issue 0** — `/types-of-sambo/` cannot be published either way until
   the head-injury claim is dealt with.
3. **If the gym does not teach Sambo:** rewrite **one** of the three as a purely
   informational article on Sambo — the technique content on
   `/sambo-techniques/` is the best-written instructional material on the site —
   strip every ownership claim, and 301 the other two into it. `/types-of-sambo/`
   is the clearest candidate to be redirected rather than rewritten: its one
   subject is already a section of `/sambo-martial-art/`. One informational page
   on a discipline the gym admires is defensible; three pages advertising an
   academy that does not exist is not.
4. **If the gym does teach Sambo:** the pages stay, but they need a programme page
   behind them, and the duplication is then worth fixing on its own merits —
   starting by merging `/types-of-sambo/` into the section it duplicates.

#### The gear pair: two posts, one primary keyword, and they disagree about the business — **CRITICAL**

`/muay-thai-gear/` and `/muay-thai-gear-for-safety/` both target **"muay thai
gear"** as their primary keyword. The slugs differ by three words; the subject
does not.

**The newer one links the older one with its own keyword as the anchor text.**
`/muay-thai-gear-for-safety/`'s very first internal link is "muay thai gear like
Fairtex shorts" pointing at `/muay-thai-gear/` — the exact phrase both pages are
trying to rank for, handed from one to the other.

**And the two pages contradict each other about what the business does.** The
older says the gym offers "**personalized gear advice**"; the newer says "**We
stock Fairtex and Yokkao pieces**" and runs eight product sections in the first
person. That is issue 74, and it makes this pair different from every other
cluster in this document: the duplication is not just wasteful, it is
*inconsistent*.

**The wider gear cluster.** Beyond the pair, the site also has
`/muay-thai-gloves/`, `/muay-thai-clothing/`, `/muay-thai-gym-bag/` and
`/muay-thai-backpack/`, plus `/kick-boxing-gloves/` and
`/mixed-martial-arts-training-gloves/`. Six of those eight pages are about an
item of equipment, and the two "gear" pages are general enough to overlap all of
them — the newer one has a Gloves section, a Shorts section and an eight-item
product list.

**What to do: 301 `/muay-thai-gear-for-safety/` into `/muay-thai-gear/`.** The
older URL is shorter, is the exact keyword, has eight months of history, and is
the page the newer one links to. Merge the newer page's eight product sections
into it **rewritten in the third person** (issue 74), and keep the four
item-specific posts as the deeper pages they already are, each linked from the
surviving hub.

Do issue 74 first either way. Consolidating two pages that disagree about whether
the gym sells equipment would just pick one answer at random.

#### The hyphen pair: the same product and the same keyword on two URLs — **CRITICAL**

`/kick-boxing-gloves/` and `/kickboxing-gloves/` are **the clearest duplicate in
this document**. They differ by one hyphen:

| | `/kick-boxing-gloves/` | `/kickboxing-gloves/` |
|---|---|---|
| Slug | kick-**boxing**-gloves | kickboxing-gloves |
| Subject | kickboxing gloves | kickboxing gloves |
| Primary keyword | "kick boxing gloves" | "Kickboxing Gloves" |
| Published | 6 Mar 2025 | 26 Dec 2025 |

Not a broad-versus-narrow overlap, not a different angle on a shared topic: the
same product, the same buyer, the same query, nine months apart. And **the newer
one links the older**, with the anchor text "finger", so the two are explicitly
connected.

**The hyphen problem is site-wide, not just this pair.** The site uses both
spellings in slugs with no rule:

| Hyphenated | One word |
|---|---|
| `/kick-boxing-gloves/` | `/kickboxing-gloves/` |
| `/kick-boxing-bag/` | `/kickboxing-techniques/` |
| `/kick-boxing-classes/` | — |

So the whole kickboxing topic is split across two URL conventions, and search
engines treat "kick boxing" and "kickboxing" as near-synonyms rather than
identical, which means neither spelling accumulates the authority a single
convention would.

**Inside the articles the newer page is actually consistent**: it never writes
"Kick boxing" or "Kick Boxing", and the only hyphenated string on it is the link to
its own twin. The hyphenated posts mix all three forms in one article (issue 33's
pattern applied to a discipline name). So the body copy has improved; the slugs
have not.

**Recommendation: 301 `/kick-boxing-gloves/` into `/kickboxing-gloves/`.** The
newer page is longer, better structured and the one-word spelling is the higher
volume form. Then pick ONE convention — "kickboxing", no hyphen — and redirect
`/kick-boxing-bag/` and `/kick-boxing-classes/` to one-word equivalents so the
topic stops being split. **Remove the "finger" anchor when you do**, or it becomes
a self-link.

**The wider gear cluster still needs consolidating too.** Alongside this pair sit
`/muay-thai-gloves/`, `/mixed-martial-arts-training-gloves/`,
`/muay-thai-gear-for-safety/` and `/muay-thai-gear/` — six pages about buying
gloves and gear for a business with no shop (issue 74). That is one decision, not
six: confirm whether equipment sales are real, then build one page or none.

#### The kidney pair: two posts on one strike, one instructional and one rules-based — **High**

`/kidney-punch/` and `/are-kidney-shots-legal-in-ufc/` (17 November 2025) both
cover kidney strikes. They are not redundant in the usual way — **they are framed
oppositely**, and that is what makes the pair worth acting on rather than just
merging:

| | `/kidney-punch/` | `/are-kidney-shots-legal-in-ufc/` |
|---|---|---|
| Framing | **instructional** | **mixed, mostly rules** |
| Headings | "How to Throw a Kidney Punch", "Step by Step Breakdown for Kidney Punching" | "UFC Rules That Directly Affect Kidney Strikes", "How Referees Judge Legal vs Illegal Strikes", "List of Relevant Fouls" |
| Step sequences | yes | **none** |
| Rules content | secondary | the spine of the article, with a referee-decision table |
| Injury risk | mentioned | described in detail across a whole H2 |
| Invisible ChatGPT links | four, one to a commercial health company | **none** |
| Disclaimer | none | **none** |

The newer post links the older under the anchor "legal in UFC".

**The newer post is the safer of the two, but it is not purely rules-based.** Five
passages describe how and from where the strike is delivered:

> "Many fighters throw a kidney shot during the clinch from an underhook after a
> takedown or when the opponent's body is slightly turned."

> "Fighters dig hard hooks under the ribcage when the opponent stands square or
> turns a little."

> "The punch drives upward into the soft flank area below the ribs."

> "A sharp kidney blow from an underhook or after turning the opponent's body
> drains stamina fast without breaking rules."

> "A low roundhouse to the body heel kick to the side or heel to the kidney from
> the side during stand up or ground exchanges is perfectly legal."

Those are positional and delivery instructions, and the "Improves Strategic Body
Targeting During Fights" H3 is explicitly tactical: "Fighters mix the kidney shot
with liver shot solar plexus attacks and rib cage work to break opponents down
slowly and legally." So the page teaches the strike incidentally while explaining
its legality — a real difference from `/kidney-punch/`, which teaches it on
purpose under how-to headings, but not the clean separation the framing of the
title suggests.

**Recommendation: keep this one, rewrite or remove `/kidney-punch/`.** The
rules-and-refereeing structure here is the right model — a reader learns what is
legal, what is a foul and what the referee does, which is defensible content for a
gym to publish. Two changes are needed before it is the model:

1. Cut the five delivery passages above, or recast them as what referees watch for
   rather than what fighters do.
2. Add the disclaimer this page also lacks (issue 31).

`/kidney-punch/` should then be 301'd into it. **Do not merge them as they stand** —
folding step-by-step striking instruction into the rules page would undo the only
thing the rules page has going for it.

#### The neck bridge pair: two strength-and-technique guides for one movement — **CRITICAL**

`/neck-bridges/` and `/do-neck-bridges-build-muscle/` (16 November 2025) are both
instructional guides to the same exercise. This is not a broad-versus-narrow
overlap; they cover the same ground in the same way:

| | `/neck-bridges/` | `/do-neck-bridges-build-muscle/` |
|---|---|---|
| Subject | the neck bridge | the neck bridge |
| Technique | step-by-step, wall-assisted and partner progressions | "Step by step back neck bridge", "Step by step front neck bridge" |
| Variations | front, back, wall | "Common variations (front, back, wrestler bridge)" |
| Programming | training plan and progressions | "Recommended reps &amp; sets", 3-4 sets, weekly progression |
| Safety | "Are neck bridges safe for beginners?" | "How to Do Neck Bridges Safely" |
| Muscles | neck and upper back | four H3s on anterior, posterior, trapezius, stabilisers |
| Disclaimer | **none** | **none** (issue 31) |

The newer post links the older, and the auto-linker split the sentence doing it:
`Do <a href="/neck-bridges/">neck bridges build </a>muscle safe for wrestler and
grapplers.` — a two-word anchor with a trailing space (issue 64).

**Both pages are already flagged for liability.** The older one carries four
invisible ChatGPT citation links, one of them to a commercial health company
(issue 40), and an "H2:" marker in a visible heading (issue 22). The newer one has
a second H1 mid-article (issue 82). Neither has a disclaimer.

**Recommendation: one page.** Merge into whichever URL has the better search
history — `/neck-bridges/` is older — take the newer post's anatomy sections and
its four medical-clearance lines, which are the best safety copy on either page,
and 301 the other. **Do the disclaimer first**: consolidating two
undisclaimered spine-loading articles produces one longer undisclaimered
spine-loading article.

#### The self-defence cluster reaches TEN, and the spelling now splits THREE ways — **CRITICAL**

`/mix-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` (20 January 2026) is the tenth, and the
cluster stays the largest group in the project by a wide margin:

| # | Post | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `/best-martial-arts/` | general |
| 2 | `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | one H2 per discipline |
| 3 | `/best-martial-arts-for-self/` | truncated slug (issue 44) |
| 4 | `/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/` | general |
| 5 | `/best-self-defense/` | general |
| 6 | `/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/` | British **defence** |
| 7 | `/top-ten-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | a numbered ten |
| 8 | `/top-fighting-styles/` | ranks one discipline (issue 80) |
| 9 | `/martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | general, with reviews |
| 10 | **`/mix-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`** | **"Mix", not "Mixed"** |

**The spelling split now runs three ways across one cluster:**

| Variant | Where | Count on the newest page |
|---|---|---|
| "self defense" (American) | 8 of the 10 slugs | throughout |
| "self defence" (British) | `/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/` | — |
| **"Mix" martial arts** | **this slug and this H1** | body uses it **once** |
| "Mixed" martial arts | the standard form | body uses it **three times** |

**The new page contradicts its own slug.** Its URL and its H1 say "**Mix**
Martial Arts". Its body says "Mix Martial Arts" exactly once — in its opening
sentence, "Best Mix Martial Arts for Self Defense help you pick the right
style" — and then uses the correct "**Mixed** Martial Arts" twice and "mixed
martial arts" once, including in its own H2, "**Mixed** Martial Arts (MMA) for
Complete Self Defense". So the page knows the right form and the URL does not.

That is a third slug-level defect, after the truncated
`/best-martial-arts-for-self/` and the misspelt
`/martial-arts-for-kids-strenght/` (issue 44). "Mix martial arts" is also a
real search term, so this one may have been deliberate — but it reads as an
error above the title in a result, and the page itself overrules it four
sentences in.

**It also links FIVE of the nine siblings**, which is the most self-citation of
any page in the cluster: `/best-martial-arts-for-self/`,
`/martial-arts-for-self-defense/`, `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`,
`/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/` and `/best-martial-arts/` — every
single internal link on the page goes to a post it competes with (issue 5).

**Recommendation unchanged from the ninth entry**, with one addition: the page to
keep is still `/best-martial-arts-for-self/`'s content on
`/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`'s slug. This tenth page should 301 there
too, and the "Mix" spelling should not be carried over.

#### The self-defence cluster reaches NINE, and two of them are one slug with the end cut off — **CRITICAL**

`/best-martial-arts-for-self/` (30 December 2025) is the ninth, and the
self-defence cluster is now **the largest group in the project**, ahead of Muay
Boran at seven and belts at six:

| # | Post | Shape |
|---|---|---|

| 3 | **`/best-martial-arts-for-self/`** | **one H3 per discipline — the truncated slug** |

| 8 | `/top-fighting-styles/` | ranks one discipline (issue 80) |
| 9 | `/martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | general, with reviews |

**Posts 2 and 3 are a near-exact duplicate pair, and the only difference between
their URLs is that one of them is cut off.** `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`
and `/best-martial-arts-for-self/` are the same article written twice:

| | `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | `/best-martial-arts-for-self/` |
|---|---|---|
| Disciplines broken out | Muay Thai, Boxing, BJJ, Krav Maga, Judo, Karate, Taekwondo, Jeet Kune Do — as **H2s** | BJJ, Muay Thai, Boxing, Wrestling, Sambo, Krav Maga, Judo, Karate, Taekwondo, Wing Chun — as **H3s** |
| Other H2s | "Introduction", "Key Points", "Main Content", "Training Regimen", "Training Methods", "Training Practices" — leaked editorial markers (issue 22) | proper section headings, no markers |
| Slug | complete | **truncated** (issue 44) |
| Words | — | 2,175 |

**The headings were compared directly and they do not match verbatim**: of this
page's 39 H2s and H3s, **1 matches a heading on
`/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` — 3% — and it is "Conclusion"**, and **3
match either of the two closest siblings — 8% — all three boilerplate** ("Contact
Us", "Conclusion", "Follow Us on Social Media"). Against
`/martial-arts-for-self-defense/` the H2-level overlap is 20%, also boilerplate.

**But seven of the same disciplines are broken out on both pages**, in the same
order of priority, answering the same query. Rewritten headings over the same
material is harder to spot than a copy-paste and no better for search: Google has
nine pages for one question and has to choose.

**Which to keep.** This page is the **best-built of the nine** — no leaked
editorial markers, ten disciplines covered rather than eight, a real FAQ, and the
only no-guarantee caveat on the site (issue 0). Its slug is the one thing wrong
with it. So: **keep this page's content at
`/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`'s slug**, 301 the other eight into it, and
retire the truncated URL.

#### The self-defence cluster reaches EIGHT, and the newest is the thinnest — **CRITICAL**

`/top-fighting-styles/` (13 November 2025) is the eighth page chasing "which
martial art is best for self-defence". The cluster, oldest first:

| Post | Published | Angle |
|---|---|---|
| `/best-martial-arts/` | 18 Feb 2025 | "Top Disciplines Ranked" |
| `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | 21 Jan 2025 | "Which Style Suits You?" |
| `/which-martial-art-is-best-for-self-defence/` | 8 Sep 2025 | the question as a title |
| `/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/` | 17 Sep 2025 | **the closest match by title** |
| `/best-self-defense/` | 17 Sep 2025 | "for Protection" |
| `/top-ten-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | 20 Sep 2025 | a ranked ten that is five (issue 65) |
| `/martial-arts-for-self-defense/` | 18 Oct 2025 | six disciplines, four untaught |
| `/top-fighting-styles/` | 13 Nov 2025 | a ranked list of one (issue 80) |

**`/best-fighting-style-for-self-defense/` is the closest match.** "Top fighting
styles" and "best fighting style for self-defense" are the same query with a
synonym swapped; both posts promise a ranked comparison of styles for real
self-defence. The older one actually delivers one. The newer ranks only BJJ.

Eight pages is the largest cannibalisation group in this document, and it has
grown by four since February. Google has to choose one of eight; none of them
accrues the authority a single strong page would, and the newest is the weakest
candidate to win.

**Recommendation: consolidate to ONE.** Pick the strongest — `/best-martial-arts/`
has the oldest URL and the broadest title — merge the comparison tables and the
discipline sections from the other seven into it, and 301 all seven. If the client
wants a second page, make it a genuinely different question ("self-defence for
women", "self-defence for kids") rather than another synonym.
`/top-fighting-styles/` is the first that should go: it has no content the other
seven lack, and issue 80 means it needs rewriting anyway.

#### The versus quartet: four comparison posts, and one pair is the same comparison written twice — **CRITICAL**

Four posts are built as head-to-head comparisons:

| Post | Comparison | Published |
|---|---|---|
| `/mma-vs-kickboxing-for-self-defense/` | MMA vs kickboxing | 17 Sep 2025 |
| `/mixed-martial-arts-vs-kickboxing/` | MMA vs kickboxing | 11 Nov 2025 |
| `/muay-thai-vs-kickboxing/` | Muay Thai vs kickboxing | 20 May 2025 |
| `/muay-thai-vs-mma/` | Muay Thai vs MMA | 16 Jun 2025 |

**The first two are the same comparison.** Not adjacent, not overlapping — the
same two disciplines, weighed against each other, under two slugs that differ
only in whether "MMA" is spelled out and whether "for self defense" is tacked on.
Between them the site now has two pages competing for "mma vs kickboxing", and
the newer one links the older under the anchor text "Kickboxing vs mma", which
tells Google the two are about the same thing in the clearest way possible.

Add the two Muay Thai posts and every pairwise comparison among MMA, kickboxing
and Muay Thai is published except Muay Thai vs BJJ. A reader who wants to know
which to train has four articles to choose between, by the same gym, that do not
reference each other's conclusions — and the two MMA-vs-kickboxing posts do not
even agree in emphasis: the newer one argues MMA is "a safer full-contact sport
overall", a claim the older one does not make.

**Recommendation: consolidate to two at most.** One "MMA vs kickboxing" page and
one "Muay Thai vs kickboxing" page, with Muay Thai vs MMA folded into whichever
is stronger. 301 the rest. Which of the two MMA-vs-kickboxing posts survives is
not a free choice: `/mixed-martial-arts-vs-kickboxing/` is the page carrying the
29 omitted safety claims in issue 0, so it cannot be the canonical version until
that is resolved, and `/mma-vs-kickboxing-for-self-defense/` is the one with the
older URL and whatever authority has accrued to it. Keep the older, fix it, and
redirect the newer into it once its copy has been dealt with.

#### The apparel trio: three posts answering "what do I wear" — **High**

`/what-to-wear-to-muay-thai-class/` (13 November 2025) is the third page on this
subject. The other two predate it:

| Post | Published | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| `/muay-thai-clothing/` | 14 Apr 2025 | "Clothes, Style Ideas & Safety Tips" — shorts, tops, what to avoid |
| `/muay-thai-gear/` | — | gear overall, which includes apparel alongside gloves and pads |
| `/what-to-wear-to-muay-thai-class/` | 13 Nov 2025 | shorts, tops, rash guards, footwear, hand wraps, gloves |

**`/muay-thai-clothing/` is the closest match**, not `/muay-thai-gear/`. Both it
and the new post are built around the same decision — what a beginner should put
on — and both work through shorts, then tops, then what not to wear.
`/muay-thai-gear/` is broader and its apparel section is one part of a larger
article, so it overlaps rather than duplicates.

The new post links both of the others, and the anchor text gives the problem away:
it links `/muay-thai-clothing/` under the anchor **"What to wear"** — its own
primary keyword, pointing at the page it competes with. That tells Google the two
pages answer the same query and that the older one is the better answer.

**Recommendation:** keep `/muay-thai-clothing/` as the canonical apparel page,
since it is seven months older and has the broader title, and either 301
`/what-to-wear-to-muay-thai-class/` into it or narrow the new post to the one
thing it does that the older does not — the beginner's first-class checklist with
hand wraps, gloves, water bottle and towel. Leave `/muay-thai-gear/` alone and
strip its apparel section down to a link.

#### The belt cluster reaches SIX, and two of them are the same keyword on two slugs — **CRITICAL**

`/bjj-belt-ranks/` (26 December 2025) is the sixth, and it makes the group worse in
one specific way: **`/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` and `/bjj-belt-ranks/` are a
near-exact duplicate pair.** Same keyword, same subject, same structure, two slugs:

| | `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` | `/bjj-belt-ranks/` |
|---|---|---|
| Slug | the phrase spelled out | the abbreviation |
| Primary keyword | "BJJ belt ranks" | "BJJ Belt Ranks" |
| Adult order | White, Blue, Purple, Brown, Black, Red and Black | White, Blue, Purple, Brown, Black |
| Beyond black | degrees, called **"dans"** | degrees, then **coral**, then **red** |
| Kids colours | grey, yellow, orange, green | white, gray, yellow, orange, green |

The whole six-page cluster:

| Post | Scope |
|---|---|
| `/martial-arts-belt-levels/` | Karate and Taekwondo orders, not BJJ |
| `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/` | BJJ order, 8-12 years to black |
| `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` | BJJ order, purple at 5 years, "dans" |
| `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/` | BJJ order, 10 years to black, coral and red with year figures |
| `/martial-arts-progression/` | a four-belt sequence that is wrong (issue 72) |
| `/bjj-belt-ranks/` | BJJ order, 10 years to black, coral and red, kids from age four |

**The newest page links three of the other five** — `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/`,
`/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` and `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/` — so the
cluster is now internally cross-linked, which tells Google all four cover the same
ground and leaves it to pick one.

**It is also the most accurate of the six.** Its adult order is right, its
beyond-black sequence is right, its kids system is right, and alone among the six it
states the kids starting age — **four** — which matches the Kids BJJ programme
page. So the content to keep is here; the URL to keep is not.

**Recommendation unchanged: `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/` is still the page to
keep**, because it is the only one of the six with a local slug and local intent is
the only thing that distinguishes any of them in search. Merge the correct belt
detail from `/bjj-belt-ranks/` into it, 301 the other five, and **remove the three
cross-links** when you do or they become self-links.

#### The belt cluster reached FIVE, and that post contradicts the others — **CRITICAL**

`/martial-arts-progression/` (16 November 2025) is the fifth page on belt
advancement. The other four are below; this one adds a fault the others do not
have — **it gets the belt order wrong, and disagrees with itself.**

In its body, twice, it states the progression as four belts:

> **Start at white belt and move to orange belt, brown belt, then black belt.**

That skips **yellow, green, blue and purple**. It is not the order in any system,
and it contradicts `/martial-arts-belt-levels/`, which gives the Karate order
correctly as "White, Yellow, Orange, Green, Blue, Purple, Brown, and Black" —
eight belts, not four.

Two headings later the same post gives the BJJ order **correctly**:

> Brazilian jiu jitsu gives stripes on white belt first. **Then belt progression to
> blue, purple, brown belt and black belt.**

So the page states two different orders, 400 words apart, and the first one uses an
**orange belt that adult BJJ does not have** — then applies it to BJJ rolling:
"Light rolls at orange belt turn heavy at brown belt." `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/`
and `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` both publish the adult BJJ order as White,
Blue, Purple, Brown, Black, which the orange-belt sentences contradict.

A reader setting expectations from this page will think there are four belts and
that orange comes second. Both are wrong, and the site's own other pages say so.

**On ages and timeframes it is clean.** The post states **no age and no belt
timeframe anywhere in its body prose** — no "ages 4 to 12", no "7 and up", no
"takes X years to blue belt". That means it does not reproduce the age conflict
already logged for `/martial-arts-for-kids-strenght/` ("ages 7 and up" against the
Kids BJJ programme's 4 to 12, with Little Champions at 4 to 7). The only figures it
publishes are in its FAQ answers — "8-10 kyu ranks before black belt, followed by
10 dan ranks", "around 20 levels from white belt to 10th dan", "Train consistently
2-3 times weekly" — and those are about Japanese styles in general, not this gym's
programmes, so they conflict with nothing.

**Recommendation.** `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/` remains the natural page to keep
if the client consolidates: it is the only one of the five with a local slug, and
local intent is the only thing distinguishing any of them. Merge the five into it,
301 the rest, **and fix the four-belt sequence before reusing any of this post's
copy** — it is the only factual error among the five.

#### The seventh belt post, and the fifth duplicate URL pair — **CRITICAL**

`/bjj-belt-ranks-explained/` (published 2026-01-20, 3,056 words) is the **seventh**
page on this site explaining how BJJ belts work:

| Page | Published | Words |
|---|---|---|
| `/martial-arts-belt-levels/` | 2025-02-22 | — |
| `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/` | 2025-03-06 | — |
| `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` | 2025-03-28 | — |
| `/martial-arts-progression/` | 2025-09-?? | — |
| `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/` | 2025-10-18 | — |
| `/bjj-belt-ranks/` | 2026-01-?? | — |
| `/bjj-belt-ranks-explained/` | **2026-01-20** | **3,056** |

**`/bjj-belt-ranks/` and `/bjj-belt-ranks-explained/` are a near-exact duplicate
pair differing only by a suffix.** Same subject, same belt sequence, same primary
keyword, two URLs. This is the **fifth** duplicate-pair pattern in the project,
after:

1. the kick-boxing hyphen — `/kick-boxing-gloves/` vs `/kickboxing-gloves/`
2. the `10-` prefix — `/muay-boran-techniques/` vs `/10-muay-boran-techniques/`
3. the `-in-coppell` suffix — four instances, most recently
   `/martial-arts-weapons-training/` vs `/martial-arts-weapons-training-in-coppell/`
4. the truncated `-for-self` slug — `/best-martial-arts-for-self/` vs
   `/best-martial-arts-for-self-defense/`
5. **the `-explained` suffix — `/bjj-belt-ranks/` vs `/bjj-belt-ranks-explained/`**

The pattern is now consistent enough to be a process problem rather than five
accidents: whoever commissions these posts is not checking the existing URL list
before choosing a slug.

**This pair is worse than a slug collision: the two pages share a title phrase.**
`/bjj-belt-ranks/` is titled "BJJ Belt Ranks Explained: Train, Progress, and
Advance at SixthSense MMA" and `/bjj-belt-ranks-explained/` is titled "BJJ Belt
Ranks Explained Your Guide to Brazilian Jiu Jitsu Progression". Both H1s open with
the identical five words, and the older page's title already contains the newer
page's entire slug. A reader who finds both in one results list cannot tell them
apart, and neither can Google.

**The new page also links two of the pages it competes with.** Of its five
editorial links, three point at belt pages in the same cluster:
`/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/`, `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/` and
`/bjj-belt-ranks/` — the last of these from the Contact section, where the anchor
text is the duplicate's own keyword. A fourth link points at
`/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-orlando/`, a city 1,000 miles away (issue 14).

**Recommendation unchanged.** Keep `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/`, fix its H1
(issue 71), and 301 the other six into it. `/bjj-belt-ranks-explained/` adds no
subject the other six do not already cover; its only distinctive content is the
kids-belt section, which is 3 paragraphs that can be merged in full.

#### The belt quartet: four posts on one sequence of seven colours — **CRITICAL**

Four pages now explain BJJ belt progression:

| Page | Published | Slug carries a local term? |
|---|---|---|
| `/martial-arts-belt-levels/` | 2025-02-22 | no |
| `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/` | 2025-03-06 | no |
| `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belt-ranks/` | 2025-03-28 | no |
| `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/` | 2025-10-18 | **yes** |

**There is one belt system and it has not changed.** White, blue, purple, brown,
black, coral, red is the same sequence on all four pages. Unlike the self-defence
sextet, where the pages at least ask the question in different words, these
cover an identical, finite, factual subject four times.

**The newest page links one of the other three twice.** `/bjj-belt-ranks-in-coppell/`
points at `/brazilian-jiu-jitsu-belts/` from two separate anchors — once in the
opening section and once from inside a customer review (issue 64). Two of its
nine internal links go to a page it is competing with.

**This one is the natural keeper, with one condition.** It is the only one of the
four with a local slug, and local intent is the only commercial reason this gym
needs a belt page at all: "bjj belt ranks" is an informational query answered
better by larger sites, while "bjj belt ranks in Coppell" is a query only this
business can win. But as published the page does not earn the slug — **its H1 and
all six of its tags omit Coppell entirely** (issue 71), and its only mention of
the town is the address in the contact block.

**What to do: fix the H1, then 301 the other three into it.**

1. Change the H1 to include Coppell — one edit, logged at issue 71.
2. Merge anything unique from the other three. `/martial-arts-belt-levels/` covers
   belts beyond BJJ, so if the client wants a general martial-arts belt page that
   is the one to keep as a second page; otherwise it folds in too.
3. 301 the retired URLs. They carry between them eight months of history, which
   is why redirecting beats deleting.
4. Check the belt timeframes on the survivor against issue 72 before it becomes
   the canonical answer.

#### The kids pair: two posts, one keyword, one audience — **CRITICAL**

`/martial-arts-for-kids/` (16 February 2025) and
`/martial-arts-for-kids-strenght/` (18 October 2025) target the same phrase for
the same reader: a parent deciding whether to enrol their child. This is not
partial overlap or a different angle. The second is the first, rewritten eight
months later and 3,379 words long.

**The newer page links the older one**, with the anchor text "martial art for
kids" — so the site itself tells Google the two are about the same thing,
without saying which should rank.

**The newer one is the stronger page** on structure (14 H2s, 31 H3s, a full NAP
block, Coppell four times) and the **weaker** on accuracy: its URL misspells
"strength" (issue 68), it states a minimum age two years above the real one
(issue 69), it spells the head coach's name wrong (issue 33), and it describes
four disciplines the gym does not teach (issue 39).

**What to do: consolidate into one page and keep the correctly spelled slug.**
`/martial-arts-for-kids/` already has the clean URL and eight months of history,
so it is the natural survivor. Merge the newer page's structure and its NAP
block into it, fix the age and the coach's name on the way in, and **301
`/martial-arts-for-kids-strenght/` into it** — which resolves issue 68 at the
same time.

Whichever survives must link `/kids-bjj-classes/`, which neither currently does.
A parent who reads either post and wants to book has no route to the booking
page from it.

#### The cluster reaches SEVEN, and two of them publish the SAME TEN TECHNIQUES word for word — **CRITICAL**

`/muay-boran-techniques/` (20 January 2026) is the seventh, and it is the
duplicate of `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` — the two slugs differ **only by the
leading "10-"**. That is the fifth instance of the qualifier pattern, after the
kick-boxing hyphen pair, the sambo-techniques city pair, the belt-ranks
abbreviation pair and the weapons-training city pair.

**The headings barely overlap, and that is misleading.** Measured directly:

| Against | H2 exact | H2+H3 exact |
|---|---|---|
| `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` | 0/12 = **0%** | 2/32 = **6%** |
| `/muay-boran/` | 1/12 = **8%** | 2/32 = **6%** |

Every match is boilerplate ("Contact US", "Conclusion", "Follow Us on Social
Media"). On the headings alone the two pages look unrelated.

**But the ten techniques are identical, name for name and word for word.** Both
pages publish a "Specific Top 10" section, and both list the same ten:

| # | Name | Description — **identical on both pages** |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Hanuman Thawai Waen | Spinning punch and piercing dagger strike to face and throat. |
| 2 | Phra Ram Yeab Longa | Double uppercuts and snapping strike to skull or chin. |
| 3 | Crocodile Tail Kick | Heel kick to thigh or leg causing dislocation. |
| 4 | Ruesi Bod Ya | Open palm defenses and push-kicks controlling distance. |
| 5 | Fake and Strike | Fake teep followed by reverse push-kick to chest. |
| 6 | Nao Taeng Krit | Shoulder and elbow attack targeting temple and neck. |
| 7 | Batha Loop Phak | Downward palm strike breaking body defense. |
| 8 | Hirun Muan Phaendin | Slam to disrupt flow and control space. |
| 9 | Hong Peek Hak | Arm dislocation technique for close combat dominance. |
| 10 | Kamae Kham Sao | Grip and piercing move to trap opponent's arm. |

**Ten for ten, with no duplicate within either list** — which is worth recording,
because `/kickboxing-techniques/` listed the hook twice in a "Top 9" and
`/sambo-moves/` padded its list. These two pages got the list right and then
published it twice.

So the duplication is in the **content**, not the headings, and it is the exact
reverse of what the heading percentages suggest. Any client checking for
duplicates by comparing section titles would clear this pair.

**The full seven-page cluster:**

| Post | Scope |
|---|---|
| `/muay-boran/` | the general explainer, cleanest slug |
| `/muay-boran-techniques/` | **techniques, by programme stage, with the Top 10 table** |
| `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` | **the same Top 10, with per-technique prose and 4 tables** |
| `/muay-boran-in-coppell/` | the local class page |
| `/muay-thai-muay-boran/` | the comparison |
| `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` | the regional styles |
| `/muay-chaiya/` | one regional style |

**Which to keep:** `/muay-boran/` for the explainer and
`/muay-boran-in-coppell/` for local intent, as recommended below. Of the two
technique pages, `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` has the richer per-technique
treatment, so merge this page's programme sections into it and 301 this slug —
**and delete one copy of the Top 10 table.** All of it conditional on the service
claim at issue 39.

#### And now a SEVENTH, and this one has the local slug — **CRITICAL**

`/muay-boran-in-coppell/` (26 December 2025) is the seventh, and it changes the
shape of the cluster rather than just adding to it. Every earlier post competes
for informational queries — "what is muay boran", "muay boran techniques", "muay
boran vs muay thai". This one competes for a **commercial local** query, "muay
boran in coppell", and repeats the cluster's shared material underneath it:

| Covered by | `/muay-boran-in-coppell/` | the other six |
|---|---|---|
| What Muay Boran is | the opening section and "Characteristics" | all six |
| Muay Boran vs Muay Thai | an H3 plus four H4s | five of the six |
| The regional styles | Chaiya, Korat and Lopburi, an H4 each | `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/`, `/muay-chaiya/`, `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` |
| Eight limbs / named techniques | an H4 | `/10-muay-boran-techniques/`, `/mae-mai-muay-thai-what-is-it/` |
| **A local class offering** | **yes — the whole point of the page** | `/muay-boran/` only, and more weakly |

**It links three of the cluster** — `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/`,
`/10-muay-boran-techniques/` and (through a Muay Thai anchor)
`/muay-thai-thai-boxing/` — so the same self-citation pattern as the sixth post
above.

**Which two to keep, if the client consolidates:** `/muay-boran/` has the
cleanest general slug and this page has the local slug, so those are the two
candidates — `/muay-boran/` for "what is muay boran" and
`/muay-boran-in-coppell/` for "muay boran classes near me". The other five
should 301 into `/muay-boran/`.

**But only if the programme is real.** Keeping the local page means keeping the
service claim at issue 39, and that claim is stronger here than anywhere else in
the library. If the gym does not teach Muay Boran, the correct consolidation is
one informational page and **no** city page at all.

#### And now a sixth, with the slug the whole cluster should have had — raised to **CRITICAL**

`/muay-boran/` was published on 17 October 2025, three months after the fifth.
It is 3,242 words, it has the cleanest slug on the site, and it covers **every
subject the other five cover**:

| Covered by | `/muay-boran/` | the other five |
|---|---|---|
| What Muay Boran is | H2 "What Is Muay Boran?" | all five |
| History and origins | two H2s and four H3s | `/muay-thai-muay-boran/`, `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` |
| Muay Boran vs Muay Thai | H2 plus two H3s | three of the five |
| The regional styles | H2 "Styles Within Muay Boran", with Chaiya and Korat sections | `/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/`, `/muay-chaiya/` |
| Named techniques | two numbered lists | `/10-muay-boran-techniques/`, `/mae-mai-muay-thai-what-is-it/` |

**It links three of the five it is competing with** — `/muay-thai-muay-boran/`,
`/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` and `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` —
which is the same pattern as the self-defence cluster above and the BJJ cluster
at issue 57: the newest page cites its own rivals as sources.

**Six URLs for one query on a site this size is the clearest cannibalisation
case in this document.** "Muay boran" is not a high-volume search; splitting it
six ways guarantees none of them accumulates authority, and the gym's internal
links are now scattered across all six.

**What to do: keep `/muay-boran/` and 301 the other five into it.**

The slug is the exact keyword and the shortest on the site. The scope is the
broadest. The structure is the best — thirteen H2s covering the full subject
where the others each take one slice. Before it can be the keeper it needs three
fixes, all logged elsewhere: the two leaked editorial instructions in its
headings (issue 22), the named-competitor table (issue 52), and the five
sentences missing the word "Whether" (issue 17).

Merge in what is genuinely unique from the others first: the **fifteen Mae Mai
techniques** from `/mae-mai-muay-thai-what-is-it/` and the **depth on Chaiya**
from `/muay-chaiya/`. Everything in the remaining three is already here.

That takes six URLs to one. It supersedes the "five become three" plan below,
which was written before this page existed.

**A fifth: `/mae-mai-muay-thai-what-is-it/`.** Mae Mai already has a section of
its own on `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` — the H3 "Mae Mai : Basic, powerful
strikes and defenses" — and is named again on `/muay-chaiya/` under "Look Mai /
Mae Mai". The new post is a dedicated article on the same concept, and it
repeats the cluster's shared material a fifth time: what the techniques are, why
they matter, and how they differ from Luk Mai.

It does bring something the others do not: a complete list of the **fifteen**
named Mae Mai techniques, where `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` gives ten and
neither of the others enumerates them at all. That list is worth keeping. The
introduction wrapped around it is the fifth copy.

**And now a fourth: `/muay-chaiya/`.** Muay Chaiya is one of the four regional
Muay Boran styles, and it already has a section of its own on
`/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` and another on
`/10-muay-boran-techniques/`. The new post is a 3,000-word article on the same
style, and it repeats the cluster's shared material again: what Muay Boran is,
how it differs from modern Muay Thai (its own H2 **and** a comparison table), the
Wai Khru rituals and the traditional gear.

A dedicated post on one regional style is defensible in itself — it is the
deepest treatment of Chaiya on the site. The problem is that it arrives as the
fourth page competing for overlapping Muay Boran queries, and the third to run a
"Muay Boran vs Muay Thai" comparison.

**What to do:** keep `/muay-chaiya/` as the deep dive on that style and
`/mae-mai-muay-thai-what-is-it/` as the techniques reference, since its list of
fifteen is the fullest on the site; merge `/muay-thai-muay-boran/` and
`/are-there-multiple-styles-of-muay-boran/` into a single "what is Muay Boran"
hub with the regional styles in it, and fold `/10-muay-boran-techniques/` into
the Mae Mai post, whose list supersedes it. That takes five URLs down to three.
301 the two retired URLs, cut the duplicated introductions from the survivors,
and have them all link up to the hub. **Medium priority** — below the stance, Kali, Martial Arts Classes and Best
Martial Arts pairs, which are the same article written twice, but above the
partial overlaps.

**The Muay Thai stance pair is the clearest case on the site, alongside the two
Martial Arts Classes posts (issue 24), and carries the same HIGH priority.**
`/muay-thai-stance/` ("Master the Perfect Muay Thai Stance for Victory") and
`/muay-thai-fighting-stance/` ("Muay Thai Fighting Stance Training Easy Drills
to Improve Your Form") are not two angles on a topic; they are the same article
written twice. Both open by defining the stance, both cover foot placement,
weight distribution, guard position, orthodox versus southpaw, common mistakes
and drills. Someone searching "muay thai stance" and someone searching "muay
thai fighting stance" want the same page, and Google has to choose between two
of them.

The newer post even links to the older one — "specialize in Muay Thai" points at
`/muay-thai-stance/` — which tells Google the two are related without telling it
which one to rank.

**What to do about this pair:** keep one URL, merge anything unique from the
other into it, and 301-redirect the retired URL to the survivor. `/muay-thai-
stance/` is the shorter, cleaner URL and the older post, so it is the natural
survivor unless the analytics say otherwise. Do not simply delete the loser: a
301 keeps whatever links and ranking history it has.

**What to do:** for each group, pick one page to rank, and merge or clearly
separate the others. Blog posts about classes should link to the program page
rather than compete with it.

### 9. Same generic FAQ reused on 13 posts — SEO, CONTENT — Medium

**"What is 1 2 3 4 5 6 in boxing?" — on a Muay Boran page.** The accordion on
`/muay-boran-techniques/` runs to nine questions, and this time the off-topic one
is not about the CIA:

| # | Question | On topic? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | "How many techniques are in Muay Boran?" | **yes** |
| 2 | "What are the basics of Muay Boran?" | **yes** |
| 3 | "What are the different styles of Muay Boran?" | **yes** |
| 4 | "What is the most powerful move in Muay Thai?" | adjacent |
| 5 | "**What is 1 2 3 4 5 6 in boxing?**" | **no — boxing punch numbering** |
| 6 | "Is Muay Boran allowed in MMA?" | **yes** |
| 7 | "Who is the father of Muay Boran?" | **yes** |
| 8 | "What are the forbidden moves in Muay Boran?" | **yes** |
| 9 | "What are the big 4 martial arts?" | no, generic |

**Seven of nine are on subject**, which is the second-best FAQ in this entry after
`/best-martial-arts-for-self/`. Question 5 is simply from another sport — the
numbered-punch convention used in boxing gyms — and has no bearing on a Thai
art that counts its limbs rather than its punches.

**Question 8 is the most important thing on the page, and it is buried.** Its
answer is the only place in the whole article where a restriction is stated: it
lists **Headbutts, Groin strikes** and **Eye gouging** among the moves modern
rules ban. The body of the page, by contrast, teaches strikes to the temple,
throat and skull with no mention of what is off-limits (issue 31). The one
paragraph that supplies the boundary sits collapsed at the bottom of an
accordion.

**What to do:** delete 5 and 9, and **move question 8's content into the body**,
near the Top 10 table where the targets are named.

**A FIFTH page asking what martial arts the CIA uses, and now the FBI as well.**
The accordion on `/mix-martial-arts-for-self-defense/` runs to eleven questions:

| # | Question | On topic? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | "Is mixed martial arts good for self-defense?" | **yes** |
| 2 | "Which art is best for self-defense?" | **yes** |
| 3 | "**What martial arts do CIA agents learn?**" | no — **the fifth instance** |
| 4 | "Which martial art works best in real fights?" | **yes** |
| 5 | "Can a boxer defeat an MMA fighter?" | no, hypothetical matchup |
| 6 | "Which sport is best for self-defense?" | **yes** |
| 7 | "**What martial art does the FBI use?**" | no — **new** |
| 8 | "What is the safest martial art?" | marginal |
| 9 | "What is the strongest self-defense?" | **yes** |
| 10 | "What is the hardest martial art to learn?" | no |
| 11 | "What martial art makes you the strongest?" | no |

**Question 3 is now on five pages**: the two Coppell pages, the two Sambo pages
and this one. Five FAQPage blocks, one question, all five competing for a query
that converts nobody. Question 7 adds the **FBI** to the pattern, and its answer
describes the Bureau's "Defensive Tactics" curriculum — federal law-enforcement
training, on a martial arts school's blog.

**Question 8 is the one to keep, and it is worth saying why.** Its answer —
"Taekwondo and Aikido are among the **safest**, with **lower injury rates** due to
less full-contact sparring" — is correctly hedged, comparative, and the only
honest safety answer in any FAQ in this audit. It is reproduced unchanged.

**What to do:** keep 1, 2, 4, 6, 8 and 9; delete 3 and 7 outright and across all
five pages; replace 5, 10 and 11 with what a prospective student asks.

**A FOURTH page asking what martial arts the CIA uses, and a firearms answer that
mentions "gun disarms and use".** The accordion on
`/martial-arts-weapons-training/` asks nine questions:

| # | Question | On topic? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | "What martial arts train with weapons?" | **yes** |
| 2 | "What is the most effective martial arts weapon?" | **yes** |
| 3 | "**Is there a martial art that uses guns?**" | **firearms** |
| 4 | "What are the 4 noble weapons?" | **yes** |
| 5 | "What are the 11 types of martial arts?" | no |
| 6 | "What are the big 4 martial arts?" | no |
| 7 | "What martial art makes you the strongest?" | no |
| 8 | "Which is stronger, Karate or jiu-jitsu?" | no |
| 9 | "**What martial arts do CIA agents learn?**" | no |

Three of the nine are genuinely about weapons, which is better than most of the
FAQs in this entry. Two problems remain.

**Question 3 puts firearms on the page, with a detail the others did not.** Its
answer says modern systems "like Krav Maga include **gun disarms and use**" — so a
page advertising weapons classes to teenagers, with no minimum age (issue 39),
carries an FAQPage-marked answer about firearm disarms. That is the **third**
weapons-adjacent page with firearms content, after
`/martial-arts-weapons-training-in-coppell/` and `/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/`.

**Question 9 is the fourth instance of one question.** "What martial arts do CIA
agents learn?" now appears on
`/martial-arts-weapons-training-in-coppell/`, `/sambo-techniques-in-coppell/`,
`/sambo-techniques-for-beginners/` and this page — **four pages, one question, all
four marked up as FAQPage**, competing with each other for a query that converts
nobody. Whatever is generating these FAQs is reaching for the same list each time.

**What to do:** keep 1, 2 and 4, delete 3 and 9 outright, and replace 5 to 8 with
what a weapons student asks — which weapons the class actually uses, whether they
are wood or foam, what age a teenager can start, and whether any gear is provided.

**Ten questions, two about Sambo, and one about how the CIA recruits informants.**
The accordion on `/sambo-techniques-for-beginners/` asks:

| # | Question | On topic? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | "Is Sambo effective in a street fight?" | **yes** |
| 2 | "Can kids do Sambo?" | **yes** — but see below |
| 3 | "What is the easiest fighting technique to learn?" | no |
| 4 | "What is the hardest fighting style to get?" | no |
| 5 | "What are the big 4 martial arts?" | no |
| 6 | "What are the top 3 best martial arts?" | no |
| 7 | "What martial arts do CIA agents learn?" | no — **the third page with this question** |
| 8 | "What martial art makes you the strongest?" | no |
| 9 | "What fighting style do bodyguards use?" | no |
| 10 | "**What is the CIA rice method?**" | **not about martial arts at all** |

**Question 10 is the most off-subject question in the entire audit.** The answer
explains the CIA's **RICE framework for recruiting intelligence assets** —
"**Reward** (financial or material incentives), **Ideology** (deep beliefs like
politics or religion), **Coercion** (threats or pressure), and **Ego** (desire
for status or recognition)". That is a page about how to pressure people into
betraying their country, published as an FAQ on a beginner martial arts page and
marked up as **FAQPage** so it is eligible to surface in search.
